`data/coverage.json`. Declarations are named without the `TernaryGoldbach.` prefix.

error, or the judged relation and how it was judged. Each text's list ends with the records whose proof has a

### Helfgott, *The ternary Goldbach problem* (arXiv:1501.05438v2)

Absent statements:

- Lemma 4.2.1, p. 57: The book calls this lemma a 'theme' and never applies it: Lemmas 4.2.2–4.2.4 are proved by listing changes to this proof. Erratum: Five slips in the proof, all harmless; the lemma holds (checked by `scripts/checks/lemma_4_2_1.py`).
- Lemma 5.2.2, p. 94: Only the odd-m version (Lemma 5.2.3) is used later; Lean redoes the proof for odd m and has no all-m lemma.
- Theorem 7.1.2, p. 138: Not formalized and not needed: Part III uses Cor 7.1.3 for η_*, which Lean derives from Cor 9.3.3 directly, not from this theorem.
- Lemma 9.1.2, p. 169: Lemma 9.1.2 serves only smoothings with η(0) ≠ 0; Lean's explicit formula admits only smoothings whose Mellin transform is holomorphic on Re s > −1, so it never needs the lemma.
- Lemma 9.2.1, p. 175: Side branch: feeds only Prop 9.2.2 and thence Thm 7.1.1, which Part III never cites. Erratum: The proof's step (9.35) fails even for ζ (δ = 0.01, T₀ = 52.9: 1.8539·10⁻³ > 1.5796·10⁻³), and for χ mod 5 with χ(2) = i the pairing ρ ↔ 1−ρ fails (ρ = ½+6.18358i is a zero, \|L(1−ρ,χ)\| = 2.817) (checked by `scripts/checks/lemma_9_2_1.py`).
- Lemma 11.2.5, p. 225: Not formalized; Lean replaces the comparison by putting max(g_y(n), h(y)) into ĝ and handling large q at each scale directly.
- Proposition 12.1.1, p. 228: Not formalized; only its proof technique is reused in Prop 12.1.2.
- Corollary 12.2.6, p. 243: Side result recorded after Cor 12.2.5 and not used later in the book; BLUEPRINT lists it under Ch 12 without status. Lean has the ingredients (MinorArcs.prop_12_2_4 with c₊ = 2, Prelim.large_sieve_opt, TernaryGoldbach.totient_mul_sum_units_lift) but no assembly.
- Lemma C.1.1, p. 297: Not needed: Lean replaces Cor C.1.3 by a trivial Chebyshev bound. The new Weil explicit formula at P (Explicit/WeilFormula.weil_formula) uses band-limited test functions with zero prime side and does not state this lemma.
- Lemma C.2.1, p. 301: Proof checked. It reduces to integer A; the full sum is C = (ζ(j)/ζ(2j))∏_{p\|m}(1+p^{−j})^{−1}.

## 6. Definitions and method

For each record take its key relation: for an item, the strongest relation of its key links;
for a step, its own relation.

| Class | Relations | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **1-to-1** | `exactMatch` | [L] states or establishes exactly this |
| **Faithful** | `closeMatch`, `broadMatch` | the same up to a named difference, or [L] proves more |
| **Partial** | `hasPart` | [L] carries part of it |
| **Deviates** | `narrowMatch`; a confirmed finding or erratum; `relatedMatch` where [L] argues differently | [L] proves less, or something else, or the text is wrong |
| **Absent** | `noMatch` | searched for, not in [L] |
| **Unjudged** | none | no completed verdict |

**"Formalized" means 1-to-1 + Faithful.** Report every percentage with its count, as
"41/52 (79%)". Never report a percentage without its denominator.

AI agents judged every relation: the numbered items on 2026-11-06 (a mapper, a verifier and a judge per batch), re-judged at the pin on 2026-11-12, and the displays and proof steps on 2026-11-12 (a mapper and an independent verifier per batch, a judge on every disagreement, and an audit of the absences). Verdicts were checked mechanically (every declaration and line range against the build at the pin, every snippet against the pinned source), by a second agent's re-search of one agreed absence in five and then of every other agreed absence, and by a random 5% sample read by the session lead, itself an agent; a claim that the text is wrong counts only when two independent skeptics confirm it and a re-run check script or the Lean backs it. No person has reviewed any verdict.

How the class of a record is taken here: an item's key links are its links of role `key` to
calr3/ternary-goldbach or Mathlib (not its links to this repository's `lean/` or to the book's PDF); with
none, a checked absence makes it Absent, and a link that only supports it counts as `relatedMatch`. A
record whose text a confirmed erratum (`data/items.json`) or text error (`text_error` in
`data/<overlay>-{steps,displays}-mapping.json`) says is wrong is Deviates whatever its relation; a
confirmed proof gap (the proof does not establish the statement, which may still be true) does not
change the class of the relation. The
`class` field of the mapping files is the class of the relation alone. A paper item takes no erratum
from its book counterpart.
